"Back compartment" — the back one would be the one farthest from the robot camera? Actually the caddy has a low front wall and higher back wall. Let me look more carefully. The caddy: outer brown box spanning col 75-600, row 330-590. Inside: dark cavities. Let me look at the low-res: the caddy in the 256 image: top-left region col 115-230, row 85-140. There's a divider visible around col 165 and col 190ish, plus a taller section at right col 190-230.

So compartments are arranged left-to-right along y. Then "back compartment" would mean... hmm, "back" relative to what? For a caddy, the compartments arranged along the long side, and "back" could mean the one furthest in +x (away from robot).

Wait but if compartments are side by side along y, none is "back". Unless the compartments are side by side along y and the "back" one is the one behind the raised wall... Actually maybe the right region rows 350-430 dark and then a brown vertical surface below is the FRONT-RIGHT wall of the caddy (outer wall) which is tall, and the interior is hidden behind. That doesn't fit either.

Let's use segmentation/back-projection to figure geometry. Better: get world coordinates of cavity floor points in each region and their heights.

The object's\ncanonical orientation decides which pocket is \"back\"/\"front\"; it is independent of\nwhere the robot sits. A caddy can be placed with its local back toward the robot.\n**How to apply:**\n- Do NOT equate \"back\" with \"far from robot (most negative x)\". Enumerate ALL\n  pockets, get each world xy, and treat the named side as a HYPOTHESIS, not a fact.\n- Use the hold-and-watch probe ([[libero-in-predicate-fires-while-grasped]]) to\n  test candidate pockets cheaply. If the deep/far pockets fail, TEST THE NEAR one\n  \u2014 even if its name (\"front\") seems to contradict the task word (\"back\").\n- Physical fit is a strong prior on the WRONG target too: here only the deep\n  pockets fit a low seat, which lured 3 attempts into them; the correct near\n  pocket is shallow and the mug just perches \u2014 and that fires.\n- Order probes to cover both local-frame interpretations (near AND far) before\n  concluding a task is kinematically hard.\n**Falsify:** a caddy/tray task where \"back compartment\" reliably maps to the\nfar-from-robot pocket across seeds, i.e. the region is defined in the world/robot\nframe not the fixture frame.\n**Related:** [[libero-in-predicate-fires-while-grasped]]\n"

  "size": 2053,
  "content": "---\nid: libero-in-predicate-fires-while-grasped\nscope: global\nkind: strategy\ntitle: Test placement targets by holding the object over them and watching libero_terminated,\n  before releasing\napplies_when: an In()/On() place task where the correct destination among several\n  look-alikes is uncertain, or where releasing risks tipping/losing the object\nsymptom:\n- wrong compartment\n- which container\n- ambiguous target\n- place predicate not firing\n- cannot re-grasp\n- tipped after release\n- in predicate\nevidence:\n  cells:\n  - 10_task_t5_s0\n  attempts: 4\n  solved_seeds:\n  - 0\nconfidence: single-shot\nrelated:\n- caddy-back-is-near-robot-pocket\n---\n\n\nLIBERO In()/On() success predicates check the OBJECT'S POSE, not the gripper\nstate, so `libero_terminated` flips the moment the held object's pose satisfies\nthe region \u2014 you do NOT have to release first.\n\n**Why:** libero_terminated mirrors the BDDL goal predicate, which is a function\nof object poses only. Releasing is irrelevant to the check (confirmed: placing a\nmug in the correct caddy pocket fired while still grasped; placing/releasing in\nthree WRONG pockets never fired).\n**How to apply:**\n- To disambiguate among candidate destinations, carry the still-grasped object\n  (gripper +1) low over each candidate and read `libero_terminated` from the\n  returned state. Move to the next candidate if it stays false \u2014 no release, no\n  re-grasp, no commitment. This turns an N-way target guess into N cheap probes.\n- Only `release` after the flag is already true (or to seat a confirmed target).\n- Corollary: a non-firing predicate with a clean, low, upright placement is\n  evidence of WRONG DESTINATION, not wrong physics \u2014 switch targets first.\n- Caveat: some tasks pair In() with a \"not in gripper\"/stack-stability term; if a\n  held probe won't fire but the geometry looks perfect, release once to check.\n**Falsify:** a place task where the predicate provably requires the gripper open\n(object released) and a correctly-posed held object does NOT fire.\n**Related:** [[caddy-back-is-near-robot-pocket]]\n"

Solved first try, no reset.\n\n## Winning technique\n1. `pi0_pick \"pick up the book\"` (max_chunks=16, lift_thresh=0.05). Pi0 grasps\n   near the TOP of the standing binder. Success signal: peak_lift ~0.07m,\n   gripper opening ~0.03 (holding, not shut). Book then hangs vertically ~0.13m\n   below the eef.\n2. `set_gripper +1 steps=8` to firm the grip.\n3. `move_to` STRAIGHT UP to z~1.22 so the hanging book base clears the caddy rim\n   (~z1.03-1.05) before any lateral motion.\n4. Traverse to over the back-compartment center in 2 hops (each |dx|<0.30):\n   mid waypoint, then final xy, both at carry z~1.21, gripper=1.\n5. `move_to` DOWN to z~1.08 with step_clip=0.015, gripper=1. It STALLS ~z1.157\n   when the book base contacts the compartment floor \u2014 that stall = seated.\n6. `release` (max_steps=20). Predicate did NOT fire yet at this point.\n7. `move_to` STRAIGHT UP to z~1.24, gripper=-1, step_clip=0.02. libero_terminated\n   flips true as the gripper clears the settled book.\n\n## Magic numbers\n- pi0_pick max_chunks=16 (band 12-20), lift_thresh=0.05.\n- carry z ~1.21-1.22 (must put book base above caddy rim z~1.03-1.05).\n- descend target z~1.08; it self-stalls ~1.157 on floor contact (do NOT force lower).\n- descend step_clip=0.015; retreat step_clip=0.02.\n- traverse hop rule: keep each move_to |dx| and |dy| < 0.30.\n- NEVER traverse laterally with the book base below the caddy rim (collides front wall).\n\n## Failure modes\n| symptom | root cause (A#) | fix |\n|---|---|---|\n| (none \u2014 solved on A1) | - | - |\n| predicate false immediately after release | gripper still occupying the opening | retreat straight up; predicate fired once gripper cleared |\n\n## Re-localization per scene\n- BOOK: standing black binder (lever-arch look, round hole on cover). Grasp via\n  `pi0_pick \"pick up the book\"` from home \u2014 Pi0 finds the top on its own. Median\n  a few hi-res pixels on the cover for its xy only if pre-positioning.\n- CADDY BACK COMPARTMENT: this caddy has a big LEFT compartment (undivided,\n  full-depth), a MIDDLE column split front/back, and a RIGHT elevated\n  compartment. The only front/back PAIR is the middle column, so \"back\n  compartment\" = the middle-REAR opening and \"front compartment\" = the small\n  middle-front one. Get its interior center with region `back_project` over the\n  dark interior pixels (this run: center ~(-0.454,-0.152); DO NOT cache \u2014 swap\n  re-randomizes, re-derive every scene). World frame here: +x = toward robot\n  (bottom of image), +y = image-right; \"back\" = smaller/more-negative x.\n- Book footprint (~8cm x 3cm) matched the compartment (~8cm y x 5cm x) at the\n  default wrist yaw, so no rotate_wrist was needed. If the held book's wide face\n  lies along the compartment's SHORT axis, rotate_wrist 90deg before descending.\n\n## Fragility flags\n- Compartment identity is the main risk. If a clean insert+release does NOT fire\n  the predicate, SUSPECT WRONG COMPARTMENT before wrong physics \u2014 re-classify the\n  middle-rear vs left/right openings in RGB.\n- Book must be lifted above the rim before traversing, or the base clips the\n  caddy front wall.\n\n## Difficulty and reliability\nSolved single-shot in 8 primitives, 1 attempt. Expected high single-shot rate;\nthe delicate parts are compartment ID and rim clearance, both handled by the\nsequence above.\n\n## Cross-refs\n- [[predicate-fires-after-gripper-retreat]]\n"
